E^{1}_{\alpha}
descending down to
{\mathrm{tr}}(E^{1}_{\alpha})
,
\{\phi_{\chi}\}_{\chi\in\widehat{E}^{1}_{\alpha}}
is a generating set. Therefore for smooth functions
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
supported on
{\mathrm{tr}}(E^{1}_{\alpha})
, by linearity and continuity, the following identity holds as long as
(\phi_{i},\phi_{\mathbbm{1}})_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}=0
for
i=1
or
2
(i.e. the constant Fourier coefficient of
\phi_{1}
or
\phi_{2}
vanishes)

(7.4.8)

\displaystyle\big({\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\phi_{1}),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\phi_{2})\big)_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}.

In other words, the operator
{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}
is unitary on the affine line with respect to the measure
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{ell}}(a){\Delta}(a)\mathbf{v}(a)\,\mathrm{d}a
for functions supported on
{\mathrm{tr}}(E^{1}_{\alpha})
with vanishing constant Fourier coefficient.

On the other hand, when
*={\alpha},{\beta}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}
and
{\alpha}\neq{\beta}
, let
\chi_{*}^{\mathrm{qd}}
be the unique nontrivial character of
E^{1}_{*}
whose square is trivial, by (
7.4.7
) and Mellin inversion, for any smooth function
\phi
on
{\mathrm{tr}}(E^{1}_{\alpha})
,

(7.4.9)

\big({\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\phi),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(\phi_{\chi_{\beta}^{\mathrm{qd}}})\big)_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}=\big({\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\phi),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\phi_{\chi_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{qd}}})\big)_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}=(\phi,\phi_{\chi^{\mathrm{qd}}_{\alpha}})_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}.

Variant for
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}

Finally we make a remark for the elliptic orthogonality of
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
over a local field
F
of residual characteristic not equal to two. Notice that there are no stability issue for
G
, up to conjugation, the maximal tori are parametrized by quadratic extensions of
F
, and the local
L
-packets for
G(F)
are singleton. We are working with irreducible admissible representations of
G(F)
with a fixed unitary central character
\chi:Z(F)=F^{\times}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
. For two irreducible admissible square-integrable (modulo the center) representations
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
of
G(F)
with the same central character
\chi
, let
\theta_{i}

(i=1,2)
be their distribution character, then the orthogonality of elliptic tempered characters show that

\int_{\overline{C}(F)^{\mathrm{ell}}}\theta_{1}(c)\overline{\theta}_{2}(c){\Delta}(c)\mathbf{v}(c){\omega}_{\overline{C}}=\bigg\{\begin{matrix}1&\pi_{1}\simeq\pi_{2}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{matrix}

Descending down the above identity to
\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}(F)^{\mathrm{ell}}
, the Gelfand-Graev character identity (
4.3.11
) shows that the following statements hold.

(7.4.10)

Fix a quadratic extension
E=E_{\alpha}
of
F
with
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}
. For a character
\chi_{\alpha}:E^{\times}_{\alpha}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
. The central character of the distribution character of the dihedral lifting
\theta_{\chi_{\alpha}}
is
\chi_{{\alpha}}|_{F^{\times}}\eta_{\alpha}
. Fix two unitary characters
\chi_{{\alpha},i}:E_{\alpha}^{\times}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}

(i=1,2)
that both do not factor through the norm map (hence the corresponding dihedral representations are discrete series, which are supercuspidal when
F
is non-archimedean) such that
\chi_{{\alpha},1}|_{F^{\times}}=\chi_{{\alpha},2}|_{F^{\times}}
,

\big({\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\phi_{\chi_{{\alpha},1}}),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\phi_{\chi_{{\alpha},2}})\big)_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}=(\phi_{\chi_{1}},\phi_{\chi_{2}})_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}=\bigg\{\begin{matrix}1&\phi_{\chi_{1}}=\phi_{\chi_{2}}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{matrix}

The identity shows that the operator
{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}
is unitary on
\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}(F)
with respect to the measure
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{ell}}(a){\Delta}(a)\mathbf{v}(a)\,\mathrm{d}a
for functions supportd on
\mathbf{c}(E^{\times}_{\alpha}/Z(F))
with vanishing Fourier coefficient for characters that factor through the norm (and modulo the center).

To be precise, following the same argument as the
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F)
case, for two smooth and compactly supported (even square-integrable modulo the center) functions
\phi_{i}
on
E_{\alpha}^{\times}
, let
\overline{\phi}_{i}
be the Mellin transform of
\phi_{i}
along the center
Z(F)
against the character
\chi
. Suppose that for
i=1
or
2
,
(\overline{\phi}_{i},\phi_{\xi\circ\mathrm{Nr}_{\alpha}})_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}=0
for any character
\xi
of
F^{\times}
such that
\xi\circ\mathrm{Nr}_{\alpha}|_{F^{\times}}=\chi
, then the following identity is true

\big({\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\overline{\phi}_{1}),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\overline{\phi}_{2})\big)_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}=(\overline{\phi}_{1},\overline{\phi}_{2})_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}.

Similarly, with the above assumption, after extracting the central character for
\overline{\phi}_{2}
, we also have the following identity

\big({\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\phi_{1}),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\overline{\phi}_{2})\big)_{{\mathfrak{c}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}=(\phi_{1},\overline{\phi}_{2})_{{\mathfrak{c}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}.

(7.4.11)

Fix
*={\alpha},{\beta}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}
and
{\alpha}\neq{\beta}
. For characters
\chi_{*}:E^{\times}_{*}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
both do not factor through the norm map and
\chi_{\alpha}|_{F^{\times}}\eta_{\alpha}=\chi_{\beta}|_{F^{\times}}\eta_{\beta}
(and hence their dihedral liftings share the same central character)

\displaystyle\big({\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}(\phi_{\chi_{\alpha}}),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(\phi_{\chi_{\beta}})\big)_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}=\bigg\{\begin{matrix}1&\text{$\chi^{2}_{*}$ factor through the norm map}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{matrix}

Notice that the above identity is parallel to the existence of the unique supercuspidal local
L
-packet with four elements in the
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F)
situation.

7.5.
Decomposition of the stable cocenter: motivation

As in last subsection we take
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
or
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
over a local field of residual characteristic not equal to two. Recall that in subsection
7.1
we have defined a total transfer map

{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}:{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})\to\bigoplus_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}

that is
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
-linear, with the
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
-module on
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
given by the homomorphism
\rho_{\alpha}^{*}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}^{\tau_{\alpha}}
. It is natural to ask the following question:

Question 7.5.1
.

Given
f\in{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
with
{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}(f)=(f_{\alpha})_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\in\bigoplus_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
enjoying the characterization in Theorem
7.1.1
, can we reconstruct
f
explicitly from the datum
(f_{\alpha})_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}
?

Roughly speaking, an answer to the above question would provide a decomposition of the stable cocenter by
F
-quadratic algebras, i.e.

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})\simeq\bigoplus_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{{\alpha}}

with
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\alpha}
being the image of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
under the section from
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
to
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\alpha}
. Before answering Question
7.5.1
, we would like to ask if the datum
(f_{\alpha})_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}
determines
f\in{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
uniquely, which is summarized as follows.

(7.5.1)

When
F={\mathbb{C}}
and hence
{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}={\mathcal{T}}_{0}
is the identity map,
f=f_{0}
;

(7.5.2)

When
F={\mathbb{R}}
, the collection of stable tempered characters from the dihedral lifting
\{\theta^{\mathrm{st}}_{\chi_{\alpha}}\}_{\chi_{\alpha},{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}
is dense in the space of stable distributions (Theorem
3.5.1
), hence by adjunction,
(f_{\alpha})_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}
is sufficient to determine
f
;

(7.5.3)

When
F
is non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic,
\{\theta^{\mathrm{st}}_{\chi_{\alpha}}\}_{\chi_{\alpha},{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}
is
not
dense in the space of stable distributions. One need to take into account of the distribution character of (twisted) Steinberg representations.

We introduce the following two subspaces of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
for
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}
.

Definition 7.5.2
.

Fix
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}
.

(1)

Let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{+}
be the subspace of functions
\phi_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
with the following properties:

(a)

If
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
, then
\langle\phi_{\alpha},\mathbbm{1}_{T_{\alpha}}\rangle=0
, i.e. the constant Fourier coefficient of
\phi_{\alpha}
vanishes;

(b)

If
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
, then
\langle\phi_{\alpha},\chi\circ\mathrm{Nr}_{{\alpha}}\rangle=0
for any
\chi
a character of
F^{\times}
;

(2)

Let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{++}
be the subspace of functions
\phi_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{+}
with the following properties. In particular the situation occurs only when
F
is non-archimedean:

(a)

If
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
, then
\langle\phi_{\alpha},\chi_{{\alpha}}^{\mathrm{qd}}\rangle=0
where
\chi^{\mathrm{qd}}_{\alpha}
is the unique nontrivial character of
E^{1}_{\alpha}
whose square is trivial;

(b)

If
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
, then
\langle\phi_{\alpha},\chi^{\mathrm{qd}}_{\alpha}\rangle=0
where
\chi^{\mathrm{qd}}_{\alpha}
runs over all the characters of
E^{\times}_{\alpha}
that do not factor through the norm map, but their square factor through the norm map.

(3)

For
J_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
, let
J_{{\alpha}}^{+}
and
J^{++}_{\alpha}
be the image of the natural projection from
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
onto
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{+}
and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{++}
through subtracting the corresponding Fourier coefficients.

We also introduce the parallel subspaces
{\mathcal{S}}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{+}
and
{\mathcal{S}}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{++}
for
{\mathcal{S}}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
.

These subspaces are introduced to avoid the overlap between dihedral liftings from different tori of
G(F)
.

When
F
is non-archimedean, for any
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
is a
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}^{\tau_{\alpha}}={\mathcal{Z}}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
-module since for abelian group the center and the cocenter are the same. As a result,
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{+}
and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{++}
are both
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}^{\tau_{\alpha}}
-modules. Following the discussion in subsection
7.1
, the homomorphism

\rho_{\alpha}^{*}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}^{\tau_{\alpha}}

equips
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
,
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{+}
and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{++}
with a
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
-module structure via Mellin transform. The same statements are true for
F
archimedean after replacing smooth and compactly supported functions by Schwartz functions.

The main theorem that we are going to establish in the next subsections is the following, which provides a decomposition for
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
parametrized by quadratic
F
-algebras.

Theorem 7.5.3
.

Let
F
be a local field of residual characteristic not equal to two, and
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
or
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
.

(1)

(Nonsplit summands)

(a)

For
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}
, consider the map

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}:{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\to{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})

\displaystyle J_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha})=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{ell}}\cdot\mathbf{v}{\Delta}\cdot{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}\bigg(\frac{\nu_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha})}{\mathbf{v}{\Delta}}\bigg)

When restricted to
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{+}
,
{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{\alpha}={\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{+}}
is a homomorphism of
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
-modules that provides a section for
{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}
. Here
\nu_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha})
is the unique function on
\pi_{\alpha}(T_{\alpha}(F))\subset{\mathfrak{c}}_{G}(F)
such that
\pi^{*}_{\alpha}(\nu_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha}))=J_{\alpha}
. Moreover, for
{\beta}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{{\alpha}\}
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta}\circ{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{\alpha}=0
;

(b)

For
J_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha})={\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}^{+}(J^{+}_{\alpha})
;

(c)

When
F
is non-archimedean, the operator
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{cus}}:={\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{+}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}^{++}_{1/2}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}^{++}_{-1/2}
gives rise to a
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
-linear isomorphism

{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{cus}}:{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{1}(F))^{\tau_{1}}_{+}\oplus{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{1/2}(F))^{\tau_{1/2}}_{++}\oplus{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{-1/2}(F))^{\tau_{-1/2}}_{++}\to{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{cus}}.

Here
{\mathcal{E}}^{++}_{\alpha}={\mathcal{E}}_{{\alpha}}|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{++}}
.

(d)

When
F
is real, the operator
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{+}
provides a
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
-linear isomorphism

{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{+}:{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{1}(F))^{\tau_{1}}_{+}\simeq{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{disc}}

Replacing
{\mathcal{H}}
by the Schwartz algebra
{\mathcal{S}}
when
F={\mathbb{R}}
, the statements are still true.

(2)

(Split summands)

Consider the map
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
from
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{0}(F))^{\tau_{0}}
to functions on
{\mathfrak{c}}(F)
that is given by the following formulas

\displaystyle J_{0}\mapsto\Bigg\{\begin{matrix}\nu_{0}(J_{0})&F={\mathbb{C}}\\
\nu_{0}(J_{0})-{\mathcal{E}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0}(J_{0})))&F={\mathbb{R}}\\
\nu_{0}(J_{0})-{\mathcal{E}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0}(J_{0})))-\sum_{{\alpha}=\pm 1/2}{\mathcal{E}}^{++}_{\alpha}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\alpha}}(\nu_{0}(J_{0}))^{++})&F\text{ non-archi.}\end{matrix}

Then the following statement is true: For any
J\in{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
with
{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}(J)=(J_{\alpha})_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}
, we have the following facts:

(a)

When
F={\mathbb{C}}
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\circ{\mathcal{E}}_{0}={\mathrm{Id}}
;

(b)

When
F={\mathbb{R}}
,

J=\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha});

(c)

When
F
is non-archimedean,

J-J^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}\cdot\mathbf{v}{\Delta}\Theta^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}=\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha})

where

•

when
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
,
J^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}
is constant given by

J^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}=\langle J,\Theta^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle=\int_{{\mathfrak{c}}(F)}J(c)\Theta^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}(c){\omega}_{{\mathfrak{c}}};

•

when
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
,
J^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}
factors through the determinant map by

J^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}(g)=\int_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}(F)}J(c,\det g)\Theta^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}(c){\omega}_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}};

Replacing
{\mathcal{H}}
by the Schwartz algebra
{\mathcal{S}}
when
F
is archimedean, the same statement is true.

When
F
is non-archimedean,
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}({\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{0}(F))^{\tau_{0}})
lands in
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
. Precisely, by the trace Paley-Wiener theorem,
\nu_{0}(J_{0})
lands in
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
since it can be realized as regular functions on
\mathrm{Irr}(G(F))
supported on principal series components only. Therefore
{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}(\nu_{0}(J_{0}))\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
.

The theorem is manifest for
F={\mathbb{C}}
. Hence in the next two subsections we will assume that
F
is either real or non-archimedean. The rest of the section will be devoted to the proof of the theorem.

7.6.
Summands associated with the nonsplit tori

In this subsection, we are going to establish Part (1) of Theorem
7.5.3
based on the elliptic orthogonality formulated in subsection
7.4
.

In the following let us establish the theorem for
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
over real or non-archimedean local field of odd residual characteristic. The situation for
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
is similar and we omit.

We first prove Part (1) (a) of the theorem. For the fact that
{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}^{+}
is a homomorphism of
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
-module, it follows from the following observation. By Mellin transform and inversion, for any nontrivial character
\chi_{\alpha}
of
E^{1}_{\alpha}
with
\phi_{\chi_{\alpha}}
given by (
4.3.4
), by the Gelfand-Graev character formula,
{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{{\alpha}}(\phi_{\chi_{\alpha}})=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{ell}}\cdot\mathbf{v}{\Delta}\theta^{{\mathrm{st}}}_{\chi_{\alpha}}
is the stable normalized elliptic character of the stable character of the dihedral lifting
\theta_{\chi_{\alpha}}^{{\mathrm{st}}}
. In particular this is also equal to the stable orbital integral of the matrix coefficient of the supercuspidal representation attached to the dihedral lifting of
\chi_{\alpha}
. Hence both the character
\chi_{\alpha}
and the stable normalized elliptic character corresponds uniquely to the (stable) supercuspidal component in
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
determined by
{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{\alpha})
. It follows that
{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{\alpha}
is
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
-linear.

It remains to show that
{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{\alpha}
is a section. For
{\beta}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{{\alpha}\}
, if
{\beta}=0
then
{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\circ{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{\alpha}=0
since the image of
{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{\alpha}
is supported on elliptic locus. For
{\beta}\neq 0
and
J^{+}_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{+}
, for any character
\chi_{\beta}
of
E^{1}_{\beta}
,

\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta}\circ{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}^{+}(J^{+}_{\alpha}),\chi_{\beta}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{\alpha}(J^{+}_{\alpha}),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(\phi_{\chi_{\beta}})\rangle=\big({\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\alpha}\bigg(\frac{\nu_{\alpha}(J^{+}_{\alpha})}{\mathbf{v}{\Delta}}\bigg),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(\phi_{\chi_{\beta}})\big)_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\begin{matrix}\langle J^{+}_{\alpha},\chi_{\beta}\rangle&{\alpha}={\beta}\\
0&{\alpha}\neq{\beta}\end{matrix}

Here
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
is the standard pairing between characters and functions, and the last identity follows from Theorem
7.4.1
, i.e. the unitarity of the Gelfand-Graev transform on elliptic locus. It follows that by Mellin inversion we have proved Part (1) (a) of the theorem.

For Part (1) (b), it follows from the fact that
J_{\alpha}-J^{+}_{\alpha}
is supported on trivial characters of
T_{\alpha}(F)
, whose distribution character for the dihedral lifting is a principal series character, and hence vanishes on the elliptic locus.

For Part (1) (c), it follows from the observation that up to a finite linear combination,
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{cus}}
are spanned by the stable orbital integral of matrix coefficients of supercuspidal representations of
G(F)
, which are equal to the stable normalized elliptic characters of supercuspidal representations of
G(F)
. Hence they are precisely given by the image of
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{cus}}
. Notice that only the subspaces
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}_{++}
for
{\alpha}=\pm 1/2
show up since the dihedral lifting from different tori of the supercuspidal representations of
G(F)
have unique overlap at the nontrivial character whose square becomes trivial. Finally Part (1) (c) follows from the same argument as the non-archimedean case, after applying Mellin transform to functions in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{1}(F))^{\tau_{1}}_{+}
.

7.7.
Summands associated with the split torus

In this subsection, we are going to establish Part (2) of Theorem
7.5.3
. Again we establish the theorem for
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
over real and non-archimedean local field of odd residual characteristic. The situation for
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
is similar and we omit.

We first show the case when
F={\mathbb{R}}
. To show that
J=\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha})
, it suffices to show that
J
and
\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha})
are the same on the split and elliptic locus separately. By the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
, as
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
has image supported on elliptic locus only, we only need to verify the equality over elliptic locus. Over the elliptic locus, by definition,
\nu_{0}(J_{0})
vanishes identically, and hence

\big(\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha})\big)|_{{\mathfrak{c}}(F)^{\mathrm{ell}}}={\mathcal{E}}_{1}(J_{1})-{\mathcal{E}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0}(J_{0}))).

Therefore it remains to show that over the elliptic locus,

J|_{{\mathfrak{c}}(F)^{\mathrm{ell}}}={\mathcal{E}}_{1}(J_{1}-{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0}(J_{0}))).

By the density of stable tempered characters, and the fact that the principal series characters are all supported on split locus, it suffices to show that for any character
\chi:T_{1}(F)\to{\mathbb{C}}
with stable lifting
\theta^{\mathrm{st}}_{\chi}={\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(\phi_{\chi})
,

(7.7.1)

\displaystyle\langle J,{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(\phi_{\chi})\rangle=\langle{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(J_{1}-{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0})(J_{0})),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(\phi_{\chi})\rangle

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
is the standard pairing between stable orbital integrals and stable characters that is given by the integration over the Steinberg-Hitchin base with respect to the usual additive Haar measure. The left hand side of (
7.7.1
), by adjunction, can be rewritten as
\langle J_{1},\chi\rangle
.

It turns out that to show (
7.7.1
), it suffices to notice that based on the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
, the right hand side can be rewritten as

\big({\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(J_{1}-{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0}(J_{0}))),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(\phi_{\chi})\big)_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}.

Moreover, the function
J_{1}-{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0}(J_{0}))
enjoys the following characterization

•

Viewed as a function on
T_{1}(F)
, the constant Fourier coefficient vanishes

\langle J_{1}-{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0}(J_{0})),\mathbbm{1}_{T_{1}}\rangle=\langle J_{1},\mathbbm{1}_{T_{1}}\rangle-\langle\nu_{0}(J_{0}),\theta_{\mathbbm{1}_{T_{1}}}^{\mathrm{st}}\rangle=0.

Notice that the last identity follows from the fact that
\theta_{\mathbbm{1}_{T_{1}}}^{\mathrm{st}}=\eta_{1}
and Part (1) of Theorem
7.1.2
.

It follows that based on Theorem
7.4.1
,

\displaystyle\big({\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(J_{1}-{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0}(J_{0}))),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(\phi_{\chi})\big)_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}=

\displaystyle\big(J_{1}-{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0}(J_{0})),\phi_{\chi}\big)_{\overline{{\mathfrak{c}}}^{\mathrm{ell}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle J_{1}-{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0}(J_{0})),\chi\rangle=\langle J_{1},\chi\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle J,\theta^{\mathrm{st}}_{\chi}\rangle=\langle J,{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(\phi_{\chi})\rangle

which is exactly equal to the left hand side. It follows that we complete the proof of the theorem for
F={\mathbb{R}}
situation.

It remains to treat the case when
F
is non-archimedean. As usual, using the density of stable tempered characters, we need to show that the left hand side and the right hand side share the same value after pairing with all the stable tempered characters of
G(F)
. Following the same idea as the archimedean case, both sides share the same value over the split locus. It suffices to verify that both sides share the same value after pairing with all dihedral stable tempered characters attached to quadratic field extensions and Steinberg characters.

First, by elliptic orthogonality,

\langle J-J^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}\cdot\mathbf{v}{\Delta}\Theta^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}},\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle=\langle\nu_{0}(J_{0}),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\langle\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha}),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle=\langle\nu_{0}(J_{0}),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle+

\displaystyle\sum_{{\alpha}\neq 0}\langle{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha}),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle-\langle{\mathcal{E}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{1}(\nu_{0}(J_{0}))),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{{\alpha}=\pm 1/2}\langle{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}^{++}({\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}(\nu_{0}(J_{0}))^{++}),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle.

By Mellin transform and inversion, up to a finite linear combination, for
{\alpha}\neq 0
, the image of
{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}
and
{\mathcal{E}}^{++}_{\alpha}
are all given by the normalized elliptic stable character of supercuspidal representations. Hence the elliptic orthogonality shows that

\displaystyle\langle\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha}),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle=\langle\nu_{0}(J_{0}),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle.

In conclusion both sides share the same paring with Steinberg character. We are left to show that both sides share the same paring with stable dihedral supercuspidal characters. The idea is similar to the archimedean case and we will use the elliptic orthogonality reformulated in Theorem
7.4.1
. For any nontrivial character
\chi_{\beta}:T_{\beta}(F)\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
with
{\beta}\neq 0
, we need to show that

\langle J,\theta_{\chi_{\beta}}\rangle=\langle\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha}),\theta_{\chi_{\beta}}\rangle

Equivalently,

\langle J,{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(\phi_{\chi_{\beta}})\rangle=\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\langle{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha}),{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{{\beta}}(\phi_{{\beta}})\rangle.

But it follows from the same argument as the archimedea case with the aid of Theorem
7.4.1
.
It follows that we complete the proof of the theorem.

Remark 7.7.1
.

Let
F
be non-archimedean. Recall that we have a decomposition of the stable cocenter
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
:

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})={\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{cus}}\oplus{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{ind}}

where the cuspidal part
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{cus}}
is supported by the union of zero dimensional (modulo the center when
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
) components of the stable Bernstein varieties and the induced part
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{ind}}
is supported by the union of positive-dimensional (modulo the center when
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
) components.
By restricting the transfer attached to the split torus
{\mathcal{T}}_{0}
to the induced part we obtain a surjective map

{\mathcal{T}}_{0}:{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{ind}}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{0}(F))^{\tau_{0}}

whose kernel is generated by the normalized elliptic character of Steinberg representation twisted by compactly supported functions along the split center, i.e.

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{St}}=\ker{\mathcal{T}}_{0}|_{{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{ind}}}=\big\{\big(\mathbf{v}{\Delta}\Theta^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}\big)\cdot\phi\circ\det\mid\phi\in{\mathcal{H}}(Z).\big\}

In particular, when
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
and hence
Z
is trivial, the above kernel is exactly given by the scalar multiples of the normalized elliptic Steinberg character. We get an exact sequence of
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
-module

(7.7.2)

0\to{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{St}}\to{\rm SO}({\mathcal{H}})_{\rm ind}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{0}(F))^{\tau_{0}}\to 0.

As a result,
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{St}}
can be viewed as the torsion part of
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathrm{ind}}
and its free part is isomorphic to the structural sheaf of the union of positive-dimensional components of the stable Bernstein variety (modulo the center when
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
).

When
F
is archimedean, one can show that the section
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
is
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
-linear, in the sense that for any
z\in{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
and
J_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{0}(F))^{\tau_{0}}
, for every tempered stable character
\Theta_{[\pi]}
the following identity holds

\langle{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(z_{0}J_{0}),\Theta_{[\pi]}\rangle={\gamma}(z,[\pi])\langle{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(J_{0}),\Theta_{[\pi]}\rangle

where
z_{0}=\rho_{0}(z)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{0}(F))^{\tau_{0}}
is the image of
z
under the descent map. However, when
F
is non-archimedean,
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
is no longer
{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\mathrm{st}}}
-linear due to the existence of Steinberg representation.

Remark 7.7.2
.

Let
F
be non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic and
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
or
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
. For the purpose of next two subsections, we introduce the following two subspaces of
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
:

Definition 7.7.3
.

(1)

Let
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\perp\mathrm{St}}
be the subspace of
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
such that the following function in
g\in G(F)
vanishes identically

J_{\mathrm{St}}(g)=\int_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}(F)}J(c,\det g)\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}(c){\omega}_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}}

(2)

Let
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\perp\mathrm{triv}}
be the subspace of
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
such that the following function in
g\in G(F)
vanishes identically

J_{\mathrm{triv}}(g)=\int_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}(F)}J(c,\det g){\omega}_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}}.

In other words,
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\perp\mathrm{St}}
(resp.
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\perp\mathrm{triv}}
) is the subspace of
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
that are orthogonal to (twisted) Steinberg characters (resp. one-dimensional characters).

Recall that the Steinberg character is given as follows (which in particular is independent of
\det g
):

(7.7.3)

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}(g)=\bigg\{\begin{matrix}\phi_{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}}({\mathrm{tr}}g,\det g)-1&\text{$g$ split}\\
-1&\text{$g$ elliptic}\end{matrix}

where
\phi_{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}}({\mathrm{tr}}g,\det g)=\frac{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}(g)+{\delta}_{B}^{-1/2}(g)}{{\Delta}(g)}
. Based on the explicit character formula for
\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}
, the contribution
J_{\mathrm{St}}^{\mathrm{ell}}
in Part (2) (c) of Theorem
7.5.3
can be rewritten as follows

(1)

For
J\in{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\perp\mathrm{St}}
,

J^{{\mathrm{ell}}}_{\mathrm{St}}(g)=\int_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}(F)}\nu_{0}(J_{0})(c,\det g)(1-\phi_{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}})(c,1){\omega}_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}}.

For abbreviation, we write the right hand side as
J^{{\mathrm{ell}}}_{\mathrm{St}}(g)=\langle\nu_{0}(J_{0}),1-\phi_{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}}\rangle_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}(g)
;

(2)

For
J\in{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\perp\mathrm{triv}}
,

J^{{\mathrm{ell}}}_{\mathrm{St}}(g)=\int_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}(F)}\nu_{0}(J_{0})(c,\det g){\omega}_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}}.

The right hand side above is abbreviated as
J^{{\mathrm{ell}}}_{\mathrm{St}}(g)=\langle\nu_{0}(J_{0}),1\rangle_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}(g)
;

In other words, for the above two situations,
J^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}
can be rewritten as a linear form in
\nu_{0}(J_{0})
. Using the fact that
\Theta^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}=-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{ell}}
, it follows that Part (2) (c) of Theorem
7.5.3
can be reformulated as follows:

Theorem 7.7.4
.

With the notation and convention in Theorem
7.5.3
, when
F
is non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic, define the following two extended sections from
J_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{0}(F))^{\tau_{0}}
to functions on
{\mathfrak{c}}(F)
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{\perp\mathrm{St}}(J_{0})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{E}}_{0}(J_{0})-\mathbf{v}{\Delta}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{ell}}\cdot\langle\nu_{0}(J_{0}),1-\phi_{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}}\rangle_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{\perp\mathrm{triv}}(J_{0})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{E}}_{0}(J_{0})-\mathbf{v}{\Delta}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{ell}}\langle\nu_{0}(J_{0}),1\rangle_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}

Then

(1)

For
J\in{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\perp\mathrm{St}}
,

J={\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{\perp\mathrm{St}}(J_{0})+\sum_{{\alpha}\neq 0}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha});

(2)

For
J\in{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\perp\mathrm{triv}}
,

J={\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{\perp\mathrm{triv}}(J_{0})+\sum_{{\alpha}\neq 0}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha}).

These two extended sections will be used in the next two subsections to construct inversion formulae for
{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\mathrm{st}},\mathrm{fin}}
and the stable orbital integral of spherical functions.

8.
Inversion formula for
{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\mathrm{st}},\mathrm{fin}}

In this section, over a local field
F
of odd residual characteristic, we provide inversion formulas for the descent formulas in Theorem
6.1.1
. The inversion formula will be an immediate corollary of Theorem
7.7.4
.

8.1.
The problem of inversion and its solution

Let
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
or
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
over a local field
F
of residual characteristic not equal to two. The dihedral lifting in subsection
4.2
yields a transfer of characters of
T_{\alpha}(F)
to stable characters of irreducible representations of
G(F)
. This gives rise to a homomorphism of algebras
\rho_{\alpha}^{*}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\mathrm{st}},\mathrm{fin}}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
(when
F
is archimedean, we work with
{\mathcal{S}}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
). The inversion problem that we are going to study in this section is the following:

Question 8.1.1
.

Given
(J_{\alpha})_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}
with
J_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}

(
resp.
{\mathcal{S}}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
for
F
archimedean
)
, under what condition can we ensure that there exists
J\in{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\mathrm{st}},\mathrm{fin}}
such that
\rho_{\alpha}^{*}(J)=J_{\alpha}
, and if one can find an explicit formula for
J
as a function on the Steinberg-Hitchin base?

For motivational purpose, let us assume that
F
is non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic. Based on Theorem
6.1.1
, the descent homomorphism
\rho_{\alpha}^{*}
factorizes as the composition
\rho_{\alpha}^{*}={\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\circ\mathrm{LafSec}
, with the Lafforgue transform
\mathrm{LafSec}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\mathrm{st}},\mathrm{fin}}\to{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
and the Langlands stable transfer
{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}:{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
. When
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
, as functions on the Steinberg-Hitchin base, the Lafforgue transform is given by the formula (
5.1.2
)

\mathrm{LafSec}({\bf c}^{*}J_{\mathfrak{c}}\,\mathrm{d}g)={\mathcal{F}}_{\psi}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi^{-1}}(J_{\mathfrak{c}})}{|.|}\right),\quad\mathbf{c}^{*}J_{\mathfrak{c}}\,\mathrm{d}g\in{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\mathrm{st}},\mathrm{fin}}

and the Langlands stable transfer is given by the Gelfand-Graev formula (
4.3.14
)

{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}(f)=\lambda_{{\alpha}}\pi_{\alpha}^{*}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{\psi}\big(\eta_{\alpha}{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi^{-1}}(f)\big)\right)\quad f\in{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}}).

Their composition, the descent to
\rho_{\alpha}^{*}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\rm st,fin}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(E_{\alpha}^{1})^{\tau_{\alpha}}
is given by the descent formula

\rho_{\alpha}^{*}({\bf c}^{*}J_{\mathfrak{c}}\,\mathrm{d}g)=\lambda_{{\alpha}}\pi_{\alpha}^{*}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{\psi}\left(\frac{\eta_{\alpha}{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi^{-1}}(J_{\mathfrak{c}})}{|.|}\right)\right).

By Theorem
5.4.1
, the image of
\mathrm{LafSec}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\mathrm{st}},\mathrm{fin}}\to{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
lands in
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\perp\mathrm{triv}}
that is orthogonal to all the one-dimensional characters of
G(F)
. On the other hand, Theorem
7.1.1
also describes the image of the total transfer to tori
{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}:{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})\to\bigoplus_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
. It turns out that the restriction of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}
to the image of the Lafforgue transform
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\perp\mathrm{triv}}
induces an isomorphism of
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
-modules to
\mathrm{im}({\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus})
. Combining Theorem
7.5.3
and Theorem
7.7.4
, we summarize the discussion in the following theorem.

Theorem 8.1.2
.

Let
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
or
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
over a local field
F
of residual characteristic not equal to two. Let
\rho_{\oplus}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\mathrm{st}},\mathrm{fin}}\to\bigoplus_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}^{\tau_{\alpha}}
be the total descent. The image of
\rho_{\oplus}
is the subspace of
\bigoplus_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}^{\tau_{\alpha}}
consisting of pairs

(J_{\alpha}\mid{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}})\in\bigoplus_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}^{\tau_{\alpha}}

satisfying the following compatibility conditions

(1)

If
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
, then

(a)

\langle J_{0},\eta_{\alpha}\rangle=\langle q_{\alpha},1_{\alpha}\rangle
for
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}
, where
1_{\alpha}
is the trivial character of
T_{\alpha}
and
\eta_{\alpha}
is the quadratic character of
F^{\times}
attached to
E_{\alpha}/F
.

(b)

\langle J_{\alpha},\chi_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{qd}}\rangle=\langle J_{\beta},\chi^{\mathrm{qd}}_{\beta}\rangle
for any
{\alpha},{\beta}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}
, where
\chi^{\mathrm{qd}}_{\alpha}
and
\chi^{\mathrm{qd}}_{\beta}
are the unique nontrivial character of
T_{\alpha}=E^{1}_{\alpha}
and
T_{\beta}=E^{1}_{\beta}
whose square is trivial.

(2)

If
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
, then

(a)

\langle J_{0},(\chi,\chi\otimes\eta_{\alpha}\rangle=\langle J_{\alpha},\chi\circ\mathrm{Nr}_{\alpha}\rangle
for
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}
, where
\chi
is a character of
F^{\times}
and
\mathrm{Nr}_{\alpha}:E^{\times}_{\alpha}\to F^{\times}
is the norm map.

(b)

\langle J_{\alpha},\chi^{\mathrm{qd}}_{\alpha}\rangle=\langle J_{\beta},\chi^{\mathrm{qd}}_{\beta}\rangle
for any
{\alpha},{\beta}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}
, where
\chi^{\mathrm{qd}}_{\alpha}
and
\chi^{\mathrm{qd}}_{\beta}
are the characters of
E^{\times}_{\alpha}
and
E^{\times}_{\beta}
such that
\chi_{\alpha}
and
\chi_{\beta}
does not factor through the norm map but their square does, and their dihedral lifting to
G(F)
are isomorphic.

For
(J_{\alpha}\mid{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}})
satisfying the above compatibility condition, there exists a unique
J\in{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\mathrm{st}},\mathrm{fin}}
such that
\rho_{\alpha}^{*}(J)=J_{\alpha}
for any
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}
. Moreover,
J=(\mathbf{c}^{*}J_{\mathfrak{c}})\,\mathrm{d}g
is given by the following formulae:

(1)

When
F={\mathbb{C}}
,

J=\mathrm{Laf}(\nu_{0}(J_{0}));

(2)

When
F={\mathbb{R}}
,

J=\mathrm{Laf}(\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha}));

(3)

When
F
is non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic,

\displaystyle J=\mathrm{Laf}\bigg({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{\perp\mathrm{triv}}(J_{0})+\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(J_{\alpha})\bigg)

Here for a function
z
on
{\mathfrak{c}}(F)
,
\mathrm{Laf}(z)={\mathcal{F}}_{\psi}\big(|\cdot|{\mathcal{F}}_{\psi^{-1}}(z)\big)
where the Fourier transforms and the absolute value norm are taken in trace variable, and the sections
{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}
and
{\mathcal{E}}^{\perp\mathrm{triv}}_{0}
are defined in Theorem
7.5.3
and Theorem
7.7.4
.

9.
Stable orbital integral of spherical functions

Another important consequence of the Bernstein decomposition of the stable cocenter is an explicit formula for stable orbital integrals of spherical functions. As a corollary we derive a formula for the stable orbital integral of the basic function.

9.1.
Unramified stable orbital integrals

Throughout the section we let
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
or
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
over a local field of residual characteristic not equal to two. Let
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{unr}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathrm{unr}}
) be the subalgebra of
{\mathcal{H}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{S}}
) that are bi-
K
-invariant, where

K=\Bigg\{\begin{matrix}G({\mathfrak{o}})&F\text{ non. archi.}\\
{\mathrm{U}}_{2}\cap G(F)&F={\mathbb{C}}\\
{\mathrm{O}}_{2}\cap G(F)&F={\mathbb{R}}\end{matrix}

is a fixed maximal compact subgroup of
G(F)
that is also open when
F
is non-archimedean. Here
{\mathrm{U}}_{2}
(resp.
{\mathrm{O}}_{2}
) is the corresponding unitary group (resp. orthogonal group) in
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
(resp.
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
).

By Satake isomorphism and its archimedean variant given by the Harish-Chandra isomorphism (
[
Sat63
]
[
Ank91
]
), we have isomorphisms

\mathrm{Sat}:{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{unr}}\quad(\text{resp. }{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathrm{unr}})\simeq{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{c}(T_{0}(F))^{\tau_{0}}_{\mathrm{unr}}\quad(\text{resp. }{\mathcal{S}}(T_{0}(F))_{\mathrm{unr}}^{\tau_{0}})

For
h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{unr}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathrm{unr}}
, let
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)
be its stable orbital integral. From
[
Sat63
, Lem. 4.3]
,

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)|_{\mathbf{c}(T_{0})}={\mathcal{T}}_{0}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h))=\mathrm{Sat}(h).

On the other hand, for
F
non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic and
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0,1\}
,

{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h))=0

which follows from the following adjoint identity for any character
\chi_{\alpha}
of
T_{\alpha}

\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)),\chi_{\alpha}\rangle=\langle{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h),\theta^{\mathrm{st}}_{\chi_{\alpha}}\rangle

and the fact that the dihedral lifting of
\chi_{\alpha}
to
G(F)
is ramified. It follows that we derive the following lemma.

Lemma 9.1.1
.

For any
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}
, let
h_{\alpha}={\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h))
. Then

(1)

h_{0}=\mathrm{Sat}(h)
;

(2)

h_{\alpha}=0
for
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0,1\}
;

(3)

When
F
is non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic,
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)\in{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\perp,\mathrm{St}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to establish Part (3) of the lemma, which follows from the fact that (twisted) Steinberg representations are all ramified.
∎

As a corollary, based on Theorem
7.5.3
and Theorem
7.7.4
, the following statements hold.

Theorem 9.1.2
.

With the notation and convention from Theorem
7.5.3
and Theorem
7.7.4
, the following statements hold: For
h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{unr}}

(
resp.
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathrm{unr}}
)
, let
h_{\alpha}={\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h))
for
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}
. Then

(1)

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)=h_{0}=\mathrm{Sat}(h)
if
F={\mathbb{C}}
;

(2)

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)={\mathcal{E}}_{0}(h_{0})
if
F={\mathbb{R}}
;

(3)

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)={\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{\perp,\mathrm{St}}(h_{0})
if
F
is non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic.

Proof.

It suffices to establish the theorem for
F\neq{\mathbb{C}}
. By Lemma
9.1.1
and Theorem
7.7.4
,

(1)

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)={\mathcal{E}}_{0}(h_{0})+{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(h_{1})
if
F={\mathbb{R}}
;

(2)

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)={\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{\perp,\mathrm{St}}(h_{0})+{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(h_{1})
if
F
is non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic.

Hence it suffices to show that
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(h_{1})=0
. By Theorem
7.5.3
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(h_{1})={\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{1}(h_{1}^{+})
. But by definition, for any character
\chi_{1}
of
T_{1}(F)
,

\langle h_{1},\chi_{1}\rangle=\langle{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h),\theta^{{\mathrm{st}}}_{\chi_{1}}\rangle

which vanishes unless
{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{1})
is an unramified representation. But this happens only when
\chi_{1}
is the trivial character for
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
, and factors through the norm map when
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
. It follows that
h_{1}^{+}=0
and hence
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(h_{1})={\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{1}(h^{+}_{1})=0
.
∎

Remark 9.1.3
.

The fact that
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)={\mathcal{E}}^{\perp,\mathrm{St}}_{0}(h_{0})
and
h_{0}={\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)|_{\mathbf{c}(T_{0})}
reflects the heuristic that, at least over the local function field, there should exists a perverse
t
-structure on the stacky quotient
[G/{\mathrm{Ad}}(G)]
, such that
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)
can be realized as the trace of Frobenius of a perverse sheaf on
[G/{\mathrm{Ad}}(G)]
which is the intermediate extension of its restriction to the split locus (
[
BKV22
]
). The question will be addressed in a forthcoming work.

9.2.
Stable orbital integral of the basic function

As a corollary to Theorem
9.1.2
, we derive an explicit formula for the stable orbital integral of the basic function on
G(F)
when
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
or
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
in turns of the basic function on the maximal tori. Notice that when
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
, to make sense of the basic function we need to enlarge it to be
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
to make sense of the unramified twist.
Recall from Conjecture
2.1.1
, for a pair
(G,\rho)
, the basic function
{\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}_{G}
is the unique spherical function on
G(F)
such that

{\mathcal{Z}}(s,{\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}_{G},\varphi^{\circ}_{\pi})=L(s,\pi,\rho)

where
\varphi^{\circ}_{\pi}
is the zonal spherical function of an unramified representation
\pi
of
G(F)
. Following the discussion in
[
Ngô20
, (4.15)]
(see also
[
Luo19
]
), the above equation can also be reformulated as follows:

(9.2.1)

For any irreducible admissible representation
\pi
of
G(F)
,
{\mathrm{tr}}\pi({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})
is nonzero if and only if
\pi
is unramified. When
\pi
is unramified, as a meromorphic function in the Satake parameter of
\pi
,

{\mathrm{tr}}\pi({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})=L(-\frac{n_{\rho}-1}{2},\pi,\rho).

Remark 9.2.1
.

Following
[
Luo19
, Defin. 1.3.1, Thm 1.3.3, Lem. 2.4.4]
, we renormalize our basic function and Fourier kernel so that for any unramified representation
\pi
,

{\mathrm{tr}}\pi({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})=L(\frac{1}{2},\pi,\rho)

and

\pi(\mathrm{J}^{\rho}_{\psi})={\gamma}(\frac{1}{2},\pi^{\vee},\rho,\psi).

Then
\mathrm{J}^{\rho}_{\psi}*({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})^{\vee}={\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}
.

Following the discussion in subsection
2.2
, through restricting
\rho
to the dual group of the split and unramified tori of
G(F)
, we let
{\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}_{1}
be the basic function attached to the split and unramified maximal tori of
G
.

The main result in this subsection is the following theorem.

Theorem 9.2.2
.

The following identities hold.

(1)

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})={\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}_{0}
if
F={\mathbb{C}}
;

(2)

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})={\mathcal{E}}_{0}({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}_{0})
if
F={\mathbb{R}}
;

(3)

{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})={\mathcal{E}}^{\perp,\mathrm{St}}_{0}({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}_{0})
if
F
is non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic.

Remark 9.2.3
.

Actually Part (3) of Theorem
9.2.2
is also true when the residual characteristic of
F
is even, since
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})
annihilates all the supercuspidal representations.

It is clear that Theorem
9.2.2
follows from the same discussion as Theorem
9.1.2
, which is an immediate corollary of the following lemma.

Lemma 9.2.4
.

For any
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}
,

{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}))=\bigg\{\begin{matrix}{\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}_{\alpha}&{\alpha}=0,1\\
0&{\alpha}\neq 0,1\end{matrix}.

Proof.

Although the basic functions are no longer compactly supported or Schwartz on
G(F)
, after multiplying a smooth and compactly supported function along the determinant, they lie in
{\mathcal{H}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}
respectively that do not affect the discussion of the stable transfer map.

For
\chi_{\alpha}
a character of
T_{\alpha}
, let
{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{\alpha})
be its (stable) dihedral lifting. By the adjoint identity

\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})),\chi_{\alpha}\rangle=\langle{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}),\theta^{\mathrm{st}}_{\chi_{\alpha}}\rangle={\mathrm{tr}}{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{\alpha})({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}),

it suffices to determine
{\mathrm{tr}}{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{\alpha})({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})
.
By
[
JL70
, §3]
and
[
JL70
, p.76]
, the dihedral lifting
{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{\alpha})
is unramified only when the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

For
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
,
{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{\alpha})
is irreducible. Moreover, it is unramified only when

(a)

{\alpha}=0
and hence
E=F\oplus F
. In this case
\chi_{0}=(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
is an unramified character of
E_{0}^{\times}
. When it is the case,
{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{0})\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{B}^{G}(\chi)
and

{\mathrm{tr}}{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{0})({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})=L(\frac{1}{2},{\mathcal{W}}(\chi),\rho)=L(\frac{1}{2},\chi,\rho);

where the last identity follows from the compatibility between the unramified local
L
-factor and automorphic induction (
[
BH06
]
);

(b)

{\alpha}=1
and hence
E_{1}
is the unique unramified quadratic extension of
F
, and
\chi_{1}=\chi\circ\mathrm{Nr}_{1}
with
\chi
an unramified character of
F^{\times}
. When it is the case,
{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{1})\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{B}^{G}(\chi,\chi\eta_{0})
and

{\mathrm{tr}}{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{1})({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})=L(\frac{1}{2},(\chi,\chi\eta_{1}),\rho);

(c)

For all other situations,
{\mathrm{tr}}{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{\alpha})({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})=0
;

As a result,

\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})),\chi_{\alpha}\rangle=\bigg\{\begin{matrix}\langle{\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}_{\alpha},\chi_{\alpha}\rangle&{\alpha}=0,1\\
0&{\alpha}\neq 0,1\end{matrix}

from which we deduce the lemma.

(2)

For
G={\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
and
\chi\times\chi_{\alpha}
a character of
F^{\times}\times E^{1}_{\alpha}
,
{\mathcal{W}}(\chi\times\chi_{\alpha})
is unramified only when

(a)

{\alpha}=0
and both

\chi
and
\chi_{0}
are unramified. When it is the case,

{\mathrm{tr}}{\mathcal{W}}(\chi)({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})=L(\frac{1}{2},\chi\times\chi_{0},\rho);

(b)

{\alpha}=1
and
\chi\times\chi_{1}
with
\chi
unramified and
\chi_{1}
trivial. When it is the case,

{\mathrm{tr}}{\mathcal{W}}(\chi)({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})=L(\frac{1}{2},\chi\times(\eta_{1}\times\mathbbm{1}),\rho);

(c)

For all other situations,
{\mathrm{tr}}{\mathcal{W}}(\chi)({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})=0
;

As a result,

\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathbb{L}}^{\rho})),\chi_{\alpha}\rangle=\bigg\{\begin{matrix}\langle{\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}_{\alpha},\chi_{\alpha}\rangle&{\alpha}=0,1\\
0&{\alpha}\neq 0,1\end{matrix}

from which we deduce the lemma.

It follows that we complete the proof of the lemma.
∎

10.
Orbital Hankel transform

In this section, we discuss the orbital Hankel transform for
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
or
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
considered by the second author (
[
Ngô20
]
). Over a non-archimedean local field, the stable Bernstein center
{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\mathrm{st}}}
(which in particular contains the
\rho
-Fourier kernel after multiplying a smooth and compactly supported function along the determinant) intertwines the Hecke algebra
{\mathcal{H}}
, which induces orbital Hankel transforms on the space of stable orbital integrals
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
. We are going to show that the orbital Hankel transform is compatible with the Langlands stable transfer. In particular, the
\rho
-orbital Hankel transform, which is the descent of the
\rho
-Fourier transform, descends to the abelian
\rho
-Fourier transform on maximal tori under the Langlands stable transfer. Moreover, based on the decomposition of the stable cocenter studied in Section
7
, we provide explicit formulas for the action of
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
on
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}({\mathcal{H}})
as functions on the Steinberg-Hitchin base.

10.1.
A commutative diagram

Let
{\mathcal{S}}^{\rho}(G(F))
be the
\rho
-Schwartz space conjectured in subsection
2.1
which is intertwined by the conjectural
\rho
-Fourier transform
{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho}_{\psi}
. In
[
Ngô20
]
, the second author asks if one plugs the global
\rho
-Schwartz functions into the Arthur-Selberg trace formula, can one establish the functional equation of the global automorphic
L
-functions attached to
(G,\rho)
from the geometric side of the trace formula.

To understand the problem one first need to understand the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}^{\rho}(G(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho}_{\psi}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{st.orb.}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}^{\rho}(G(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{st.orb.}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\rho}(G(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}_{\psi}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\rho}(G(F))}

Here the vertical arrows are given by taking the stable orbital integrals, and
{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\rho}(G(F))
is defined to be the space of stable orbital integrals of the conjectural
\rho
-Schwartz functions with the normalization introduced in subsection
3.2
. The lower horizontal arrow is given by the induced transform
{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}_{\psi}
, which we refer to the
\rho
-orbital Hankel transform
. The goal of this section is to show that the operator
{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}_{\psi}
is well-defined for
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
(actually
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
) or
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
, and show that the following diagram is commutative for any étale quadratic
F
-algebra
E_{\alpha}
with
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}
,

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\rho}(G(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}_{\psi}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\rho}(G(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}^{\rho}(T_{\alpha}(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho}_{\psi,T_{\alpha}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}^{\rho}(T_{\alpha}(F))}

where
{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho}_{\psi,T_{\alpha}}
is the abelian
\rho
-Fourier transform on
T_{\alpha}
constructed in subsection
2.2
. This indicates the compatibility between the
\rho
-Hankel transform and the Langlands’ stable transfer.

Remark 10.1.1
.

Here the kernel function for
{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho}_{\psi,T_{\alpha}}
is given by
\widetilde{\mathrm{J}}^{\rho}_{\psi,{\alpha}}=\mathrm{J}^{\rho}_{\psi,{\alpha}}\cdot\kappa^{\rho}_{\alpha}
rather than
\mathrm{J}^{\rho}_{\psi,{\alpha}}
which is introduced in subsection
2.4
. The constant
\kappa^{\rho}_{\alpha}
is needed due to the discrepency between the Langlands local gamma factors for dihedral liftings.

Following Conjecture
2.1.1
and Remark
2.1.2
, up to multiplying a smooth and compactly supported function on the determinant factor, we may substitute
{\mathcal{S}}^{\rho}(G(F))
by
{\mathcal{S}}(G(F))
which is the space of test functions when
F
is non-archimedean, and the space of rapidly decreasing functions when
F
is archimedean. We also substitute the
\rho
-Fourier kernel by a stable distribution
J\in{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
.

The following theorem is the main result of this subsection.

Theorem 10.1.2
.

Fix
J\in{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{st}}
. Consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}(G(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J}
st. orb.
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}(G(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
st. orb.
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}(G(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{H}}_{J}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}(G(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}(T_{\alpha}(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}(T_{\alpha}(F))}

where
{\mathcal{S}}(G(F))={\mathcal{H}}
the space of smooth and compactly supported functions on
G(F)
when
F
is non-archimedean, and is the space of Schwartz algebra
{\mathcal{S}}
when
F
is archimedean. The upper
(
resp. lower
)
horizontal operator is given by

h\in{\mathcal{S}}(G(F))\mapsto J*h^{\vee}\qquad(\text{resp. }h_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{S}}(T_{\alpha}(F))\mapsto J_{\alpha}*h_{\alpha}^{\vee})

where
J_{\alpha}=\rho_{\alpha}^{*}(J)\in{\mathcal{Z}}_{\alpha}^{\tau_{\alpha}}
. Then the following statements hold:

(1)

The operator
{\mathcal{H}}_{J}
is well-defined, and hence the top square is commutative;

(2)

The bottom square is commutative.

Proof.

Notice that by the discussion in subsection
7.1
, indeed,
{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}(G(F)))\subset{\mathcal{S}}(T_{\alpha}(F))^{\tau_{\alpha}}
.

(1) To show that the operator
{\mathcal{H}}_{J}
is well-defined, it suffices to show that for any
h\in{\mathcal{S}}(G(F))
, if
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)=0
identically, then
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(J*h^{\vee})=0
identically. Notice that
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)=0
if and only if
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h^{\vee})=0
. By Theorem
3.5.1
, the stable tempered characters are dense in the space of stable distributions, hence it suffices to show that for any tempered local
L
-packet
[\pi]
of
G(F)
with stable tempered character
\Theta_{[\pi]}^{{\mathfrak{c}}}
as a function on
{\mathfrak{c}}(F)
, the following term vanishes identically

\langle\Theta^{{\mathfrak{c}}}_{[\pi]},{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(J*h^{\vee})\rangle=\int_{g\in G(F)}\Theta^{\mathfrak{c}}_{[\pi]}(\mathbf{c}(g))J*h^{\vee}(g)\,\mathrm{d}g={\gamma}(J,[\pi])\langle\Theta^{\mathfrak{c}}_{[\pi]},{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h^{\vee})\rangle

Here the last identity follows from the fact that the regular function
{\gamma}(J,\cdot)
is constant on tempered local
L
-packets, which is established in the proof of Theorem
3.5.1
. But by definition
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h^{\vee})=0
identically, it follows that
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(J*h^{\vee})=0
.

(2) For any
f={\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)\in{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}(G(F))
, we compute

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\circ{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)={\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\circ{\mathcal{H}}_{J}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)).

By Part (1), the above term is equal to

\displaystyle={\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\circ{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(J*h^{\vee}).

For any character
\chi_{\alpha}
of
T_{\alpha}
,

\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\circ{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(J*h^{\vee}),\chi_{\alpha}\rangle=\langle{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(J*h^{\vee}),\theta^{\mathrm{st}}_{\chi_{\alpha}}\rangle={\gamma}(J,{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{\alpha}))\langle{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h^{\vee}),\theta^{\mathrm{st}}_{\chi_{\alpha}}\rangle.

By definition,
{\gamma}(J,{\mathcal{W}}(\chi_{\alpha}))={\gamma}(J_{\alpha},\chi_{\alpha})=\langle J_{\alpha},\chi_{\alpha}\rangle
with
J_{\alpha}=\rho_{\alpha}^{*}(J)
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\langle{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h^{\vee}),\theta^{\mathrm{st}}_{\chi_{\alpha}}\rangle=\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h^{\vee})),\chi_{\alpha}\rangle

But by definition, as a function on the torus
T_{\alpha}
,

{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h^{\vee}))=\big({\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h))\big)^{\vee}

it follows that we arrive that the following identity

\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\circ{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(J*h^{\vee}),\chi_{\alpha}\rangle=\langle J_{\alpha},\chi_{\alpha}\rangle\cdot\langle\big({\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h))\big)^{\vee},\chi_{\alpha}\rangle.

Applying the Mellin inversion in
\chi_{\alpha}
, we deduce Part (2) of the theorem.
∎

10.2.
An explicit formula

Theorem
10.1.2
shows that the following diagram is commutative

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}(G(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{H}}_{J}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}(G(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}(T_{\alpha}(F))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}(T_{\alpha}(F))}

In other words, for any
f\in{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{O}}(G(F))
,
{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)
enjoys the following characterization:

(10.2.1)

For any étale quadratic
F
-algebra
E_{\alpha}
with
{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}
,

{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\circ\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)=J_{\alpha}*\big({\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}(f)\big)^{\vee}.

Following the notation from Theorem
7.5.3
,
\big(J_{\alpha}*\big({\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}(f)\big)^{\vee}\big)_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}
is the image of the total transfer
{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)
. From Theorem
7.5.3
, we can derive an explicit formula for
{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)
from the datum
\big(J_{\alpha}*\big({\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}(f)\big)^{\vee}\big)_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}
, which is summarized in the following theorem.

Theorem 10.2.1
.

With the notation from above and Theorem
7.5.3
, the following statements hold:

(1)

When
F={\mathbb{C}}
,

{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)=\nu_{0}\big(J_{0}*{\mathcal{T}}_{0}(f)^{\vee}\big);

(2)

When
F={\mathbb{R}}
,

{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)=\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}\big(J_{\alpha}*({\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}(f))^{\vee}\big);

(3)

When
F
is non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic,

{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)={\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{\rm HK}(J_{0},f)+\sum_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}\backslash\{0\}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}\big(J_{\alpha}*({\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}(f))^{\vee}\big)

where

{\mathcal{E}}^{\rm HK}_{0}(J_{0},f)={\mathcal{E}}_{0}\big(J_{0}*({\mathcal{T}}_{0}(f))^{\vee}\big)-\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}\cdot\mathbf{v}{\Delta}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{ell}}.

The function
\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}
is the pull-back along the determinant
(
which in particular is constant when
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
)
of the following functions:

•

When
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
,

={\gamma}(J_{0},{\delta}_{B}^{1/2})\langle f,\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle-\langle\nu_{0}\big(J_{0}*{\mathcal{T}}_{0}(f)^{\vee}\big),\phi_{{\delta}^{1/2}_{B}}-1\rangle;

•

When
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
,

=(J_{0}{\delta}_{B}^{1/2})*\langle f,\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}-\langle\nu_{0}\big(J_{0}*{\mathcal{T}}_{0}(f)^{\vee}\big),(\phi_{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}}-1)\rangle_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}

where the convolution
*
is taken on
F^{\times}
, and
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}
is defined above Theorem
7.7.4
.

As a result,
{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)
is expressed as the abelian datum
\{(J_{\alpha}*{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}(f))^{\vee}\}_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}
. For the situation of
\rho
-orbital Hankel transforms,
{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}_{\psi}(f)
is expressed as the abelian Fourier transforms
\{{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho}_{\psi,T_{\alpha}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}(f))\}_{{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{I}}}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
7.5.3
, it suffices to treat the case when
F
is non-archimedean of odd residual characteristic. In particular, we only need to compute

\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}.

We first treat the case when
G={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
. By definition and the explicit formula for the Steinberg character from (
7.7.3
),

\displaystyle\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}=\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f),\Theta^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle=\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle-\langle\nu_{0}\big(J_{0}*{\mathcal{T}}_{0}(f)^{\vee}\big),\phi_{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}}-1\rangle.

Write
f={\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h)
, then

{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)={\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(J*h^{\vee})

and hence

\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle={\gamma}(J,\mathrm{St})\langle{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}(h),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle={\gamma}(J_{0},{\delta}_{B}^{1/2})\langle f,\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle,

In conclusion

\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}={\gamma}(J_{0},{\delta}_{B}^{1/2})\langle f,\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle-\langle\nu_{0}\big(J_{0}*{\mathcal{T}}_{0}(f)^{\vee}\big),\phi_{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}}-1\rangle.

Next let us take
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
so that
\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)^{{\mathrm{ell}}}_{\mathrm{St}}
is the pull-back of a function on
F^{\times}
along the determinant map. For any character
\chi
of
F^{\times}
, the Mellin transform of
\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)^{{\mathrm{ell}}}_{\mathrm{St}}
against the character can be rewritten as follows

\displaystyle\langle\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)^{{\mathrm{ell}}}_{\mathrm{St}},\chi\rangle=\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f),\Theta^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}\otimes\chi}\rangle=\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}\otimes\chi}\rangle-\langle\nu_{0}\big(J_{0}*{\mathcal{T}}_{0}(f)^{\vee}\big),(\phi_{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}}-1)\otimes\chi\rangle

Following the same reason as before,

\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f),\Theta_{\mathrm{St}\otimes\chi}\rangle={\gamma}(J_{0},{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}\otimes\chi)\langle f,\Theta_{\mathrm{St}\otimes\chi}\rangle.

In conclusion,

\displaystyle\langle\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)^{{\mathrm{ell}}}_{\mathrm{St}},\chi\rangle={\gamma}(J_{0},{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}\otimes\chi)\langle f,\Theta_{\mathrm{St}\otimes\chi}\rangle-\langle\nu_{0}\big(J_{0}*{\mathcal{T}}_{0}(f)^{\vee}\big),(\phi_{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}}-1)\otimes\chi\rangle.

Taking the Mellin inversion in
\chi
we get

\displaystyle\big({\mathcal{H}}_{J}(f)\big)^{\mathrm{ell}}_{\mathrm{St}}=\big((J_{0}{\delta}_{B}^{1/2})\circ\det\big)*\langle f,\Theta_{\mathrm{St}}\rangle_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}-\langle\nu_{0}\big(J_{0}*{\mathcal{T}}_{0}(f)^{\vee}\big),(\phi_{{\delta}_{B}^{1/2}}-1)\rangle_{{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}}.

∎

10.3.
Global speculation

Finally we make a brief remark for the global speculation for the
\rho
-orbital Hankel transform. This subsection is largely conjectural.

Following the last section of
[
Ngô20
]
, we fix a pair
(G,\rho)
where
G
is a reductive group over a
global
field
k
, and
\rho
is a representation of the Langlands dual group of
G
.

For any local place
\nu\in|k|
, following Conjecture
2.1.1
, there should exists a pair
({\mathcal{S}}^{\rho}(G(k_{\nu})),{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho}_{\psi_{\nu}})
of
\rho
-Schwartz space and a
\rho
-Fourier transform intertwining the
\rho
-Schwartz space. One can form the global
\rho
-Schwartz space

{\mathcal{S}}^{\rho}(G({\mathbb{A}}_{k}))=\bigotimes^{\prime}_{\nu\in|k|}{\mathcal{S}}^{\rho}(G(k_{\nu}))\curvearrowleft{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho}_{\psi}=\bigotimes_{\nu\in|k|}{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho}_{\psi,\nu}

where the right hand side is the restricted tensor product with respect to the
\rho
-basic functions
\{{\mathbb{L}}^{\rho}_{\nu}\}_{\nu\in|k|}
. The
\rho
-Poisson summation conjecture proposed in
[
BK00
]
predicts that the following identity holds

\sum_{{\gamma}\in G(k)}h(x{\gamma}y^{-1})=\sum_{{\gamma}\in G(k)}{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho}_{\psi}(h)(y{\gamma}x^{-1}),\quad h\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\rho}(G({\mathbb{A}}_{k}))

at least when we put mild local constraints to the function (see
[
Luo
, Conj. 5.3.1]
also). In
[
Ngô20
]
, the second author suggests to take
x=y
and integrate both sides of the Poisson summation identity against the automorphic quotient of
G
, which, by trace formula, would give us a rough expansion of the following form. Here
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{cus}}
is the set of cuspidal automorphic representations of
G
and
L(\pi,\rho)
is the global automorphic
L
-function for the pair
(G,\rho)
,
k^{\mathrm{qd}}/k
runs over all the global quadratic algebras of
k
,
{\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}_{h}(e)
is the global stable orbital integral of
h
evaluated at
e\in(k^{\mathrm{qd}})^{\times}
, and
{\mathrm{vol}}(e)
is the Tamagawa number for the elliptic torus attached to
e
when
e
is elliptic

\displaystyle\int_{x\in[G]}\sum_{{\gamma}\in G(k)}h(x{\gamma}x^{-1})\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\bigg\{\begin{matrix}\text{Spectral expansion:}&\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{cus}}}L(\pi,\rho)+\text{ (Extra)}\\
\text{Geometric expansion:}&\sum_{k^{\mathrm{qd}}/k}\sum_{e\in(k^{\mathrm{qd}})^{\times}}{\mathrm{vol}}(e){\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}_{h}(e)+\text{ (Extra)}\end{matrix}

By no means the above expression can be an exact identity, as one need to analyze the contribution from (Extra), which usually come residual spectrum and Eisenstein contribution on the spectral side, and endoscopic groups from the geometric side.

In
[
Ngô20
]
, as a Corollary of the conjectural
\rho
-Poisson summation, the second author asks if one can establish the following Poisson summation identity for the
\rho
-orbital Hankel transform, which should be simpler than the original
\rho
-Poisson summation conjecture, but still provides a nontrivial symmetry for the geometric side of the trace formula and hence deduce the meromorphic continuation and functional equation of the automorphic
L
-functions
L(\pi,\rho)
on the spectral side:

(10.3.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{k^{\mathrm{qd}}/k}\sum_{e\in(k^{\mathrm{qd}})^{\times}}{\mathrm{vol}}(e){\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}_{h}(e)+(\text{Extra})\sim\sum_{k^{\mathrm{qd}}/k}\sum_{e\in(k^{\mathrm{qd}})^{\times}}{\mathrm{vol}}(e){\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}_{\psi}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}_{h})(e)+(\text{Extra})

Based on the commutative diagram established in subsection
10.1
, after applying the (global) Langlands stable transfer
{\mathcal{T}}_{k^{\mathrm{qd}}}
, indeed we have the following Poisson summation identity for each fixed
k^{\mathrm{qd}}/k
,

\sum_{e\in(k^{\mathrm{qd}})^{\times}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k^{\mathrm{qd}}}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}_{h})(e)\sim\sum_{e\in(k^{\mathrm{qd}})^{\times}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k^{\mathrm{qd}}}\big({\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}_{\psi}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}_{h})\big)(e)

which follows from the
\rho
-Poisson summation identity for the
\rho
-abelian Fourier transform for
({\mathcal{S}}^{\rho}(T_{\alpha}({\mathbb{A}}_{k})),{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho}_{\psi,T_{\alpha}})
, and hence the following identity holds

(10.3.2)

\displaystyle\sum_{k^{\mathrm{qd}}/k}\sum_{e\in(k^{\mathrm{qd}})^{\times}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k^{\mathrm{qd}}}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}_{h})(e)\sim\sum_{k^{\mathrm{qd}}/k}\sum_{e\in(k^{\mathrm{qd}})^{\times}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k^{\mathrm{qd}}}\big({\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}_{\psi}({\mathrm{S}}{\mathrm{O}}_{h})\big)(e).

As a result, with a sufficiently sophisticated inversion formula for the total transfer
{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}
, we might dream that the identity (
10.3.2
) can be used to deduce the conjectural Poisson summation in (
10.3.1
). In other words, the conjectural Poisson summation for the
\rho
-orbital Hankel transform might be deduced from the abelian one in (
10.3.2
). In Theorem
7.5.3
and Theorem
10.2.1
, we provide one inversion formula for
{\mathcal{T}}_{\oplus}
, yet more sophisticated inversion formulas are to be explored.
